<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which reaction best describes the catalytic activity associated with this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → 1-acyl-sn-glycerol 3-phosphate + CoA<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Glycerol + acyl-CoA → 2-monoacylglycerol + CoA<br><br><strong>C.</strong> sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → 2-acyl-sn-glycerol 3-phosphate + CoA<br><br><strong>D.</strong> sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → phosphatidic acid + CoA<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Protein Sequence Analysis:**<br>- The protein is approximately 550 amino acids long with multiple hydrophobic/transmembrane regions, consistent with a membrane-bound enzyme.<br>- The N-terminal region contains hydrophobic segments (DVKDRSNHTVAADLDGTLLISRSAFPYYFLVALEAGSLLRALILLVSVPFVYLTYLTISETLAINVFVFITFAGL) typical of multi-pass transmembrane proteins.<br>- Key catalytic motifs observed: &quot;NHRTVLDP&quot; (containing conserved His, Asn, and Asp residues) which are characteristic of the MBOAT (MemBound O-AcylTransferase) family.<br>- The pattern of conserved residues (His, Asn, Asp) in the context of transmembrane segments is a hallmark of acyltransferases in the MBOAT superfamily.<br><br>**Evaluating the Options:**<br>- **Option B** (Glycerol + acyl-CoA → 2-monoacylglycerol): This is a monoglyceride acyltransferase reaction, not consistent with the substrate (glycerol-3-phosphate) expected for this enzyme family.<br>- **Option C** (sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → 2-acyl-sn-glycerol 3-phosphate): This would be an AGPAT/LPAAT reaction (sn-2 acylation). However, the MBOAT family GPAT enzymes specifically catalyze sn-1 acylation.<br>- **Option D** (sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → phosphatidic acid): Phosphatidic acid requires acylation at BOTH sn-1 and sn-2 positions, so this cannot be a single catalytic step.<br>- **Option A** (sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → 1-acyl-sn-glycerol 3-phosphate + CoA): This is the GPAT reaction (sn-1 specific acyltransferase), which is the first committed step in glycerolipid biosynthesis. The MBOAT family features (transmembrane domains, conserved H/N/D residues) are consistent with GPAT activity.<br><br>The protein&#x27;s architecture (membrane-associated, multiple TM domains, conserved MBOAT catalytic motifs) and the reaction specificity point to GPAT activity acting on the sn-1 position of glycerol-3-phosphate.<br><br>2. answer: A</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Compare the substrate-to-product changes in the candidate equations. Which net reaction best represents the catalytic activity of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → 1-acyl-sn-glycerol 3-phosphate + CoA<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Glycerol + acyl-CoA → 2-monoacylglycerol + CoA<br><br><strong>C.</strong> sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → 2-acyl-sn-glycerol 3-phosphate + CoA<br><br><strong>D.</strong> sn-Glycerol 3-phosphate + acyl-CoA → phosphatidic acid + CoA<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> catalytic_activity_0003:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Protein Identification:**<br>The protein sequence is 452 amino acids long, contains an N-terminal mitochondrial targeting sequence (rich in charged residues: K, R, E, D at the beginning), and has multiple transmembrane domains. These features match human AGPAT2 (1-acylglycerol-3-phosphate O-acyltransferase 2, UniProt O95165), which is exactly 452 aa and is a mitochondrial inner membrane protein.<br><br>**Catalytic Motif Analysis:**<br>The sequence contains characteristic MBOAT (Membrane-Bound O-Acyltransferase) domain motifs:<br>- &quot;NHPPPPPKPG&quot; (~position 248-258) — contains the conserved Asn-His pair<br>- &quot;LVCNHRTV&quot; (~position 305-315) — another N-H catalytic motif<br>These His-Asn residues form the catalytic site of the acyltransferase reaction.<br><br>**Catalytic Specificity:**<br>AGPAT2 belongs to the MBOAT family and is characterized as a **sn-2 acyltransferase** (EC 2.3.1.51). It acylates at the sn-2 position. The canonical reaction uses 1-acyl-sn-glycerol-3-phosphate as substrate, but AGPAT2 also has demonstrated activity on glycerol-3-phosphate, acylating it at the **sn-2 position** to produce 2-acyl-sn-glycerol-3-phosphate.<br><br>**Evaluating choices:**<br>- A: Describes sn-1 acylation (GPAT activity) — incorrect position<br>- B: Describes acylation of free glycerol — not the substrate<br>- C: Describes sn-2 acylation of glycerol-3-phosphate → 2-acyl-sn-G3P — **correct position specificity for AGPAT2**<br>- D: Would require two acyl transfers (doesn&#x27;t balance stoichiometrically)<br><br>2. answer: C</td>
